**Ghost discipline paid off — three coupled defects, not one**: (1) the known blocker (`!{Rand[mode=seeded|crypto]}` cannot call the bare `std/rand` wrappers; explicit `mode=os` passes); (2) the rejection prints **`Missing effects:` with an EMPTY payload** (message built from a label-set difference; a mode mismatch has identical labels); (3) **the recorded premise is FALSE** — enforcement is **one-directional**: an os caller silently accepts a `seeded` *or* `crypto` callee (`c3`/`c7`/`c8` exit 0) while the reverse rejects. Control `c4` proved effects DO propagate, so those passes are real. Mechanism read from the types, not inferred: `declaredEffects map[string][]string` built via `ast.EffectNames` (names only) → `stringSliceToEffectRow` **cannot** carry mode params, so the required row is **mode-blind** for every declared-function call. Naively relaxing (1) without closing (3) would make seeded/crypto declarations **vacuous** — the letter of Mark's decision, minus the replay guarantee. Controller measured breakage of the `c3`/`c7`/`c8` flip: **ZERO** in-repo `.ail` declares a non-default mode outside comments.

event[0]. Verified `go test ./internal/effects -race -count=20` (green, 111s). Commit `2bb3de2c5` →
dev CI + Build-and-Release both green (Gate 3b, bounded poll). Weekly rotation (Gate 5): #329 (53
